SUCCESSFUL DECOMPOSITION REFERENCE PACK
========================================

Purpose
-------
This file is a reusable prompt and reference pack for a Codex agent working on a
SwarmBench/MAS hierarchical task. It was assembled from approved samples and
higher-scoring task runs in this workspace. It intentionally excludes the
friend's low-scoring NMFS task and other clearly weak/failed runs.

How to use this file
--------------------
Give the prompt below to the friend's Codex agent. Tell the agent to read this
whole file before creating the task's decomposition.yaml. The YAML blocks at the
end are examples, not templates to copy blindly. The agent must adapt every ID,
source path, batch, dependency, output path, and domain requirement to the
current task.

PROMPT FOR THE FRIEND'S CODEX AGENTS
====================================

You are the decomposition architect for the current SwarmBench/MAS task.

Your immediate deliverable is a valid, high-quality decomposition.yaml for the
task that is mounted in the workspace. Do not start the substantive research or
produce the final answer until the decomposition is designed, checked, and
saved in the exact task directory expected by the harness.

1. Inspect before designing
---------------------------
Read the complete task specification, instruction.md, rubric/rulebook files,
input_artifacts, source manifest, required output schema, and any verifier
notes. Inspect the repository's existing decomposition.yaml examples and follow
the schema that the current harness actually accepts. Do not guess field names,
agent types, paths, or output locations.

Write down privately:
- every required final artifact and every required row/record/entity;
- every input artifact and source boundary;
- the natural unit of work (case, country, station, system, paper, decision,
  record pair, slide, or other task-specific unit);
- the exact final schema, allowed vocabulary, evidence/quotation rules, and
  provenance requirements;
- any rate limits, cutoff dates, or source restrictions.

2. Build a real hierarchical DAG
---------------------------------
Use a clear hierarchy with an orchestrator, parallel specialists or batch
managers, and one or more reducers/coordinators. Use dependencies to express
the true execution order. Every dependency must name an existing task ID; there
must be no cycles, orphaned tasks, accidental serial bottlenecks, or duplicate
ownership.

Prefer this general shape when it fits:

orchestrator
  -> discovery/index or source-inventory task
  -> bounded staging/fetch/normalization tasks when needed
  -> disjoint specialist or batch-manager tasks
  -> specialist leaf workers for atomic units
  -> group/region/domain coordinators
  -> final validator/reducer

For tasks with a large source set, partition by a stable, explicit key and list
the assigned keys in each description. Do not make every worker read the entire
corpus. Do not let two workers own the same item. Do not split a unit at a
boundary that makes the required judgment impossible.

3. Requirements for every sub-task
-----------------------------------
Each sub-task must have:
- a unique stable ID;
- the exact accepted agent-type/role field;
- an explicit parallel group where the schema supports it;
- a complete depends_on list;
- a narrow scope with named inputs or assigned IDs;
- one substantive responsibility;
- an exact output path and output format;
- the rules for evidence, citations, provenance, and preserving source text;
- a handoff contract that downstream agents can consume without guessing.

Descriptions must say what the worker owns, what it must read, what it must
write, what it must not do, and who consumes the result. Avoid vague tasks such
as "research this topic" or "summarize everything."

4. Use the right architecture for the task family
--------------------------------------------------
Knowledge/research and legal decision tasks:
- create an index/discovery step;
- assign non-overlapping source batches to specialist readers;
- require one normalized record per source;
- use group leads for rollups and cross-source comparisons;
- use a final reducer to assemble the exact required register, dossier, or
  evidence graph;
- keep claims tied to the source that supports them.

Data-analysis tasks:
- create the station/entity/index and source-alignment layer first;
- partition the data into disjoint geographic, temporal, or entity blocks;
- make workers compute and preserve the exact required metrics;
- use regional/domain coordinators to validate coverage, vocabulary, and
  mechanism reasoning;
- make the final reducer check row counts, formulas, units, and schema.

Planning/operations tasks:
- separate discovery from fetching and semantic reading;
- use a small staged fetch pool when the source host is rate-limited;
- stage extracted text locally before wide parallel analysis;
- assign batch managers disjoint system IDs;
- let each manager spawn one reader per assigned system;
- merge rows only after all assigned readers finish;
- finish with a readiness/risk validator and final synthesis.

Browsing, creative, and specialist tasks:
- partition the requested cases/assets/questions into atomic workers;
- preserve exact asset and evidence requirements;
- use a coordinator for continuity, consistency, or comparison;
- make the final node enforce the requested presentation/output contract.

5. Protect score-critical details
---------------------------------
Do not optimize for agent count alone. Optimize for complete coverage,
correct dependencies, traceable evidence, and a final artifact that exactly
matches the verifier.

Before saving the file, check all of the following:
- every required input unit is assigned exactly once;
- every required output field is owned by a worker or coordinator;
- all leaf outputs have a real consumer;
- every coordinator depends on all and only its assigned children;
- all final outputs are produced by a final reducer/validator;
- paths are absolute or task-relative exactly as required by the task;
- IDs, role names, output formats, and vocabulary match the contract;
- quotations, metrics, citations, and provenance are preserved exactly where
required;
- no worker is asked to invent missing evidence or rely on another worker's
unwritten assumptions;
- the YAML parses successfully and the harness can discover it.

6. Final operating procedure
----------------------------
First inspect the current task and the examples in this file. Then draft the
decomposition. Validate the YAML syntax and dependency graph. Re-read it as a
verifier: trace each required deliverable from source input to final output.
Only after the decomposition passes those checks should the agents execute the
task. If a run scores poorly, diagnose coverage, ownership, dependency,
evidence, and schema failures before changing the domain content.

Do not copy the example task IDs or paths below. Reuse the structural ideas
only. Do not mention this reference pack in the final task output.

REFERENCE YAML FILES
====================

The following blocks are full decomposition.yaml contents from approved or
successful/high-scoring runs. Duplicate snapshots of identical content are
listed together and included only once.


--- REFERENCE 01 ---
Source file(s):
  documentations_phase_2\approved_sample_tasks\019f12670f93731697b118fa22752ed0-SWARMBENCH-SPECIALIST-DATAANALYSIS-SOVEREIGN-RESILIENCE-DIAGNOSTIC\decomposition.yaml
```yaml
version: "1.0"
coordination_pattern: specialist-routing
orchestrator_directive: >
  You are the orchestrator only. Dispatch sub-agents with the task tool and nothing else.
  Do not use bash, read, grep, or write yourself. Graded files are ONLY under /logs/agent/
  per instruction.md.

  EIGHT WAVES (21 task calls from you). EVERY response MUST contain one or more task() calls.
  A message with text only and no task() call ends the run and fails the task. Never write
  "Now dispatching Wave N" without task() calls in that same message.

  Wave 1 (parallel, explore): specialist--macro-fiscal, specialist--health,
    specialist--education, specialist--poverty-inequality, specialist--governance,
    specialist--climate-environment
  Wave 2 (parallel, explore): validator--domain-macro, validator--domain-health,
    validator--domain-education, validator--domain-poverty, validator--domain-governance,
    validator--domain-climate
  Wave 3 (parallel, explore): builder--cohort-gaps, builder--source-audit
  Wave 4 (explore): reviewer--harmonization
  Wave 5 (parallel, explore): builder--composite-index, builder--summary-metrics
  Wave 6 (parallel, explore): builder--diagnostic-report, builder--charts-and-animation
  Wave 7 (explore): reviewer--coverage-qa
  Wave 8 (explore): reducer--package-assembly

  When Wave 2 validators return, your next message must be task() for Wave 3 only (no prose).
  Continue through Wave 8. You are done only when reducer--package-assembly returns.
sub_tasks:
- id: specialist--macro-fiscal
  depends_on: []
  parallel_group: domain-specialists
  description: >
    Scope: macro-fiscal domain for all 20 countries per /input_artifacts/diagnostic_brief.md
    and instruction.md. Output: /workspace/parts/macro.md, /workspace/parts/gaps_macro.csv,
    and complete /logs/agent/domain_macro.csv (all 20 countries, scoring-year rows only).
- id: specialist--health
  depends_on: []
  parallel_group: domain-specialists
  description: >
    Scope: health domain for all 20 countries per diagnostic_brief.md and instruction.md.
    Output: /workspace/parts/health.md, /workspace/parts/gaps_health.csv, and complete
    /logs/agent/domain_health.csv (all 20 countries, scoring-year rows only).
- id: specialist--education
  depends_on: []
  parallel_group: domain-specialists
  description: >
    Scope: education domain for all 20 countries per diagnostic_brief.md and instruction.md.
    Output: /workspace/parts/education.md, /workspace/parts/gaps_education.csv, and complete
    /logs/agent/domain_education.csv (all 20 countries, scoring-year rows only).
- id: specialist--poverty-inequality
  depends_on: []
  parallel_group: domain-specialists
  description: >
    Scope: poverty domain for all 20 countries per diagnostic_brief.md and instruction.md.
    Output: /workspace/parts/poverty.md, /workspace/parts/gaps_poverty.csv, and complete
    /logs/agent/domain_poverty.csv (all 20 countries, scoring-year rows only).
- id: specialist--governance
  depends_on: []
  parallel_group: domain-specialists
  description: >
    Scope: governance domain for all 20 countries per diagnostic_brief.md and instruction.md.
    Output: /workspace/parts/governance.md, /workspace/parts/gaps_governance.csv, and complete
    /logs/agent/domain_governance.csv (all 20 countries, scoring-year rows only, brief WGI
    codes in indicator_code column, source=75 in every URL).
- id: specialist--climate-environment
  depends_on: []
  parallel_group: domain-specialists
  description: >
    Scope: climate domain for all 20 countries per diagnostic_brief.md and instruction.md.
    Output: /workspace/parts/climate.md, /workspace/parts/gaps_climate.csv, and complete
    /logs/agent/domain_climate.csv (all 20 countries, scoring-year rows only).
- id: validator--domain-macro
  depends_on: [specialist--macro-fiscal]
  parallel_group: domain-validate
  description: >
    Scope: confirm /logs/agent/domain_macro.csv covers all 20 countries with scoring-year
    rows per instruction.md. Output: /workspace/parts/valid_macro.md.
- id: validator--domain-health
  depends_on: [specialist--health]
  parallel_group: domain-validate
  description: >
    Scope: confirm /logs/agent/domain_health.csv covers all 20 countries with scoring-year
    rows per instruction.md. Output: /workspace/parts/valid_health.md.
- id: validator--domain-education
  depends_on: [specialist--education]
  parallel_group: domain-validate
  description: >
    Scope: confirm /logs/agent/domain_education.csv covers all 20 countries with scoring-year
    rows per instruction.md. Output: /workspace/parts/valid_education.md.
- id: validator--domain-poverty
  depends_on: [specialist--poverty-inequality]
  parallel_group: domain-validate
  description: >
    Scope: confirm /logs/agent/domain_poverty.csv covers all 20 countries with scoring-year
    rows per instruction.md. Output: /workspace/parts/valid_poverty.md.
- id: validator--domain-governance
  depends_on: [specialist--governance]
  parallel_group: domain-validate
  description: >
    Scope: confirm /logs/agent/domain_governance.csv covers all 20 countries with scoring-year
    rows, brief WGI codes, and source=75 URLs per instruction.md.
    Output: /workspace/parts/valid_governance.md.
- id: validator--domain-climate
  depends_on: [specialist--climate-environment]
  parallel_group: domain-validate
  description: >
    Scope: confirm /logs/agent/domain_climate.csv covers all 20 countries with scoring-year
    rows per instruction.md. Output: /workspace/parts/valid_climate.md.
- id: builder--cohort-gaps
  depends_on: [specialist--macro-fiscal, specialist--health, specialist--education,
               specialist--poverty-inequality, specialist--governance,
               specialist--climate-environment]
  parallel_group: cohort-build
  description: >
    Scope: merge /workspace/parts/gaps_*.csv and build cohort metadata per instruction.md.
    Output: /logs/agent/cohort.csv and /logs/agent/data_gaps.csv (at least 34 rows, one row
    per missing country-indicator pair or missing year; do not expand into thousands of rows).
- id: builder--source-audit
  depends_on: [specialist--macro-fiscal, specialist--health, specialist--education,
               specialist--poverty-inequality, specialist--governance,
               specialist--climate-environment]
  parallel_group: cohort-build
  description: >
    Scope: read the six domain_*.csv files under /logs/agent/ and write /logs/agent/source_audit.csv
    with columns iso3, domain, indicator_code, year, source_url (exactly one audit row per
    domain CSV data row).
- id: reviewer--harmonization
  depends_on: [validator--domain-macro, validator--domain-health, validator--domain-education,
               validator--domain-poverty, validator--domain-governance,
               validator--domain-climate, builder--cohort-gaps, builder--source-audit]
  parallel_group: reconcile-validate
  description: >
    Scope: validate all six domain CSVs, cohort.csv, data_gaps.csv, and source_audit.csv under
    /logs/agent/ per instruction.md. Output: /workspace/parts/harmonized.md.
- id: builder--composite-index
  depends_on: [reviewer--harmonization]
  parallel_group: synthesis
  description: >
    Scope: composite index with the exact columns from instruction.md, two-decimal scores, ranks,
    strongest and weakest domains. Output: /logs/agent/composite_index.csv and
    /workspace/parts/index.md.
- id: builder--summary-metrics
  depends_on: [builder--cohort-gaps, builder--source-audit]
  parallel_group: synthesis
  description: >
    Scope: summary counts per instruction.md. Output: /logs/agent/summary.csv.
- id: builder--diagnostic-report
  depends_on: [builder--composite-index, builder--summary-metrics]
  parallel_group: synthesis-deliverables
  description: >
    Scope: diagnostic_report.md per instruction.md under /logs/agent/ using composite_index.csv
    with labeled two-decimal lines and ## Data Coverage Caveats citing specific data_gaps.csv rows.
    Output: /logs/agent/diagnostic_report.md only.
- id: builder--charts-and-animation
  depends_on: [builder--composite-index, builder--summary-metrics]
  parallel_group: synthesis-deliverables
  description: >
    Scope: charts and animation per instruction.md under /logs/agent/ aligned with
    composite_index.csv and domain CSVs. Output: /logs/agent/charts/resilience_ranking.png,
    /logs/agent/charts/domain_heatmap.png, and /logs/agent/resilience_race.mp4.
- id: reviewer--coverage-qa
  depends_on: [builder--diagnostic-report, builder--charts-and-animation,
               builder--composite-index, builder--summary-metrics]
  parallel_group: qa
  description: >
    Scope: cross-file consistency audit per instruction.md including two-decimal composite
    scores, gap row counts, and report label parity with composite_index.csv.
    Output: /workspace/parts/qa.md.
- id: reducer--package-assembly
  depends_on: [builder--diagnostic-report, builder--charts-and-animation,
               reviewer--coverage-qa]
  parallel_group: reduce
  description: >
    Scope: apply qa.md fixes and confirm every filename from instruction.md exists under
    /logs/agent/ before returning.
```

--- REFERENCE 02 ---
Source file(s):
  documentations_phase_2\approved_sample_tasks\0e4009871bff4666a10d1468ad1a422b-SWARMBENCH-HIERARCHICAL-BROWSING-TAXCOURT-OUTCOMES\decomposition.yaml
```yaml
sub_tasks:
- id: orchestrator--manifest-router
  depends_on: []
  parallel_group: discovery
  agent_type: general
  description: "You are the Manifest Router. Fetch the cohort manifest from https://raw.githubusercontent.com/olayis/taxcourt-outcomes-corpus/main/manifest.json, whose opinions array lists the cohort, each with id, caption, docket, dateFiled, source_url, and text_url. Split the opinions into six contiguous batches by manifest order (batch-a through batch-f, roughly equal in size). Record the routing in /workspace/routing.md as six sections, one per batch, each listing the opinions assigned to it with their id, source_url, and text_url. Do not read the opinions yourself."
- id: lead--batch-a
  depends_on: [orchestrator--manifest-router]
  parallel_group: batch-managers
  agent_type: general
  description: "You are the Batch-A Manager. From /workspace/routing.md, take only the opinions under batch-a, and record one assignment per opinion under /workspace/assign/batch-a/ (one file per slot from 01 upward) giving that opinion id, source_url, and text_url, so every leaf worker in your batch picks up exactly one opinion. If your batch holds fewer opinions than slots, leave the spare slots empty. Do not read the opinions yourself."
- id: lead--batch-b
  depends_on: [orchestrator--manifest-router]
  parallel_group: batch-managers
  agent_type: general
  description: "You are the Batch-B Manager. From /workspace/routing.md, take only the opinions under batch-b, and record one assignment per opinion under /workspace/assign/batch-b/ (one file per slot from 01 upward) giving that opinion id, source_url, and text_url, so every leaf worker in your batch picks up exactly one opinion. If your batch holds fewer opinions than slots, leave the spare slots empty. Do not read the opinions yourself."
- id: lead--batch-c
  depends_on: [orchestrator--manifest-router]
  parallel_group: batch-managers
  agent_type: general
  description: "You are the Batch-C Manager. From /workspace/routing.md, take only the opinions under batch-c, and record one assignment per opinion under /workspace/assign/batch-c/ (one file per slot from 01 upward) giving that opinion id, source_url, and text_url, so every leaf worker in your batch picks up exactly one opinion. If your batch holds fewer opinions than slots, leave the spare slots empty. Do not read the opinions yourself."
- id: lead--batch-d
  depends_on: [orchestrator--manifest-router]
  parallel_group: batch-managers
  agent_type: general
  description: "You are the Batch-D Manager. From /workspace/routing.md, take only the opinions under batch-d, and record one assignment per opinion under /workspace/assign/batch-d/ (one file per slot from 01 upward) giving that opinion id, source_url, and text_url, so every leaf worker in your batch picks up exactly one opinion. - id: lead--batch-e
  depends_on: [orchestrator--manifest-router]
  parallel_group: batch-managers
  agent_type: general
  description: "You are the Batch-E Manager. From /workspace/routing.md, take only the opinions under batch-e, and record one assignment per opinion under /workspace/assign/batch-e/ (one file per slot from 01 upward) giving that opinion id, source_url, and text_url, so every leaf worker in your batch picks up exactly one opinion. If your batch holds fewer opinions than slots, leave the spare slots empty. Do not read the opinions yourself."
- id: lead--batch-f
  depends_on: [orchestrator--manifest-router]
  parallel_group: batch-managers
  agent_type: general
  description: "You are the Batch-F Manager. From /workspace/routing.md, take only the opinions under batch-f, and record one assignment per opinion under /workspace/assign/batch-f/ (one file per slot from 01 upward) giving that opinion id, source_url, and text_url, so every leaf worker in your batch picks up exactly one opinion. If your batch holds fewer opinions than slots, leave the spare slots empty. Do not read the opinions yourself."
- id: worker--batch-a-01
  depends_on: [lead--batch-a]
  parallel_group: workers
  agent_type: explore
  description: "You are a leaf worker for batch-a, slot 01. Read your assignment under /workspace/assign/batch-a/01.txt; if there is none, you have nothing to do, so stop. Otherwise fetch the opinion text from its text_url, read it in full, and record a per-opinion JSON file at /workspace/records/<id>.json holding these fields: opinion_id, caption, taxpayer_relief (exactly one of none, partial, or substantial, read from the court's reasoning across the contested issues and not the boilerplate disposition line: none means the Commissioner prevails on every contested issue the court decides; partial means each side wins at least one contested issue; substantial means the taxpayer wins the principal issue or the relief sought), penalty_outcome (exactly one of no_penalties, upheld, rejected, or split: no_penalties means none were at issue; upheld means all were sustained; rejected means none were sustained; split means some were and some were not), principal_issue, dispositive_sentence (the verbatim sentence stating the holding on the principal issue, copied word for word and not the boilerplate line), authoring_judge, tax_years, amount_at_issue, source_url. Confirm the record is well formed and that dispositive_sentence appears in the opinion. Do not record fields for an opinion you could not read."
- id: worker--batch-a-02
  depends_on: [lead--batch-a]
  parallel_group: workers
  agent_type: explore
  description: "You are a leaf worker for batch-a, slot 02. Read your assignment under /workspace/assign/batch-a/02.txt; if there is none, you have nothing to do, so stop. Otherwise fetch the opinion text from its text_url, read it in full, and record a per-opinion JSON file at /workspace/records/<id>.json holding these fields: opinion_id, caption, taxpayer_relief (exactly one of none, partial, or substantial, read from the court's reasoning across the contested issues and not the boilerplate disposition line: none means the Commissioner prevails on every contested issue the court decides; partial means each side wins at least one contested issue; substantial means the taxpayer wins the principal issue or the relief sought), penalty_outcome (exactly one of no_penalties, upheld, rejected, or split: no_penalties means none were at issue; upheld means all were sustained; rejected means none were sustained; split means some were and some were not), principal_issue, dispositive_sentence (the verbatim sentence stating the holding on the principal issue, copied word for word and not the boilerplate line), authoring_judge, tax_years, amount_at_issue, source_url. Confirm the record is well formed and that dispositive_sentence appears in the opinion. Do not record fields for an opinion you could not read."
```

Source file(s):
  documentations_phase_2\approved_sample_tasks\1ba2ad24143646a08efde195b04331f2-SWARMBENCH-HIERARCHICAL-CREATIVEVIZ-FINANCE-PRINCIPLES-SLIDES\decomposition.yaml
```yaml
orchestrator_directive: >
  You are the lead orchestrator of a 20-chapter hierarchical slide-building job. There are 20
  per-chapter slide-writer sub-agents plus one completion-QA agent (the "queen"). Your single most
  important responsibility is to drive the entire job to completion without ending your turn early.
  Do NOT write any chapter slides or assemble the deck yourself.

  The book is pre-sliced per chapter at /opt/book_chapters/chapter_NN.txt (zero-padded, e.g. chapter
  7 -> /opt/book_chapters/chapter_07.txt), so writers read one small text file instead of the ~30MB
  PDF (if /opt/book_chapters/UNAVAILABLE exists, writers fall back to
  /input_artifacts/Principles_Finance.pdf). Each writer saves its slides to
  /logs/agent/chapter_NN_slides.json (zero-padded), first slide titled 'Chapter N: <the chapter's
  real title>'.

  How to run the job:
  1. Dispatch the 20 chapter writers in TWO large waves: chapters 1-10 together, then chapters
     11-20 together. Send all ten task calls of a wave in a single turn. Wait for the wave to
     return, then immediately dispatch the next wave.
  2. Do not stop, pause, summarize, or hand back control between waves. After the first wave
     finishes, your very next action must be to dispatch the second wave. If you ever notice you
     have dispatched writers for fewer than all 20 chapters, you are not done - dispatch the
     remaining chapters now.
  3. After both waves are done, dispatch the completion-QA "queen" sub-agent. It audits all 20
     slide files, authors any that are missing or empty itself, and then builds the final deck. You
     do not assemble the deck yourself - the queen does.
  4. You are not finished until /logs/agent/output.pptx exists, is non-empty, and covers all 20
     chapters. Confirm it. If it is missing or incomplete, re-dispatch the queen, then confirm
     again. only then may you end your turn.
sub_tasks:

    exists, read Chapter 1 from /input_artifacts/Principles_Finance.pdf instead). Decide the
    chapter's genuinely major key points - its core concepts, key definitions and formulas, the
    important models, and the main takeaways - and lay them out as at most 5 slides of concise,
    specific bullet points (the chapter's real terms/models/formulas, not vague filler). Cover only
    Chapter 1 and stay strictly faithful to the book: do not invent facts or import outside
    material. Save your slides to /logs/agent/chapter_01_slides.json as {"chapter": 1, "title":
    "<the chapter's real title from the book>", "slides": [{"title": "...", "bullets": ["...",
```

--- REFERENCE 05 ---
Source file(s):
  documentations_phase_2\approved_sample_tasks\25cc0388667e4a948b99360b7ef33266-SWARMBENCH-SPECIALIST-PLANNING-OPERATIONS-IT-VENDOR-COMPLIANCE-AUDIT\decomposition.yaml
```yaml
version: "1.0"
coordination_pattern: "specialist-routing"

sub_tasks:

  - id: sla-performance-specialist
    parallel_group: specialists
    depends_on: []
    subagent_type: explore
    description: >-
      You are the SLA and Performance specialist. Your job is to verify the
      uptime commitments and SLA claims for all 25 vendors listed in
      instruction.md.

      For each vendor, find their trust or compliance page and locate their
      published uptime SLA percentage. Then find their public status history
      page to see whether historical uptime data supports the claim. Copy a
      verbatim excerpt of at least 20 words from the source page that states
      the SLA commitment.

      Vendors to check: Slack, Zoom, Atlassian, Notion, HubSpot, Dropbox,
      Salesforce, ServiceNow, Okta, DocuSign, Box, Monday.com, Workday,
      Zendesk, Twilio, Snowflake, Datadog, Cloudflare, PagerDuty, Elastic,
      Confluent, MongoDB, GitLab, Airtable, Figma.

      Write /workspace/findings_sla.json as a JSON array with one object per
      vendor containing: vendor_name, sla_claim (the uptime % they commit to),
      sla_evidence_url (URL of the vendor's public status history or uptime page
      where historical availability data can be verified â€” for most vendors this
      is status.[vendor].com, [vendor].statuspage.io, or a /availability path
      under their trust page; this must be the live status or uptime history page,
      NOT the vendor's marketing page, trust homepage, or generic /legal page),
      sla_supported (true / false / unverified based on whether the status page
      history actually supports the claimed uptime %),
      verbatim_excerpt (20+ word direct quote from the status history page or
      the SLA terms page that states the uptime commitment),
      notes (any discrepancy between claimed % and actual history).

      Focus only on SLA and uptime. Do not check certifications, privacy,
      incidents, or contract terms â€” those are handled by other specialists.

  - id: security-cert-specialist
    parallel_group: specialists
    depends_on: []
    subagent_type: explore
    description: >-
      You are the Security Certification specialist. Your job is to verify
      the security certification claims for all 25 vendors against public
      certification registries.

      For each vendor, find their trust or security page to identify which
      certifications they claim (SOC 2, ISO 27001, FedRAMP, CSA STAR, etc.).
      Then verify each claimed certification against the relevant public registry.
      Search for the official FedRAMP authorized products list, the CSA STAR
      registry, and ISO 27001 certified clients list to cross-check vendor claims.
      Do not rely solely on what the vendor's own page says. Copy a verbatim
      excerpt of at least 20 words from the registry or vendor page that states
      the certification.

      Vendors to check: Slack, Zoom, Atlassian, Notion, HubSpot, Dropbox,
      Salesforce, ServiceNow, Okta, DocuSign, Box, Monday.com, Workday,
      Zendesk, Twilio, Snowflake, Datadog, Cloudflare, PagerDuty, Elastic,
      Confluent, MongoDB, GitLab, Airtable, Figma.

      Write /workspace/findings_certs.json as a JSON array with one object per
      vendor containing: vendor_name, certs_claimed (array of certification
      names), cert_registry_status (Verified / Expired / Not found / Unverified
      â€” one entry per cert), registry_evidence_url (URL to the specific registry
      entry or search result),
      verbatim_excerpt (20+ word direct quote from the registry or vendor page),
      notes.

      If a vendor claims a certification but it cannot be found in any public
      registry, mark it "Not found". Focus only on security certifications.
      Do not check SLA, privacy, incidents, or contract terms.

  - id: privacy-compliance-specialist
    parallel_group: specialists
    depends_on: []
    subagent_type: explore
    description: >-
      You are the Data Privacy Compliance specialist. Your job is to verify
      the GDPR and CCPA compliance claims for all 25 vendors against current
      regulatory guidance.

      For each vendor, find their privacy or data processing pages and identify
      what GDPR and CCPA commitments they publish. Check whether these
      commitments align with current regulatory guidance from the ICO for GDPR
      and the FTC for CCPA â€” search for the current official guidance from each
      regulator to verify vendor claims are consistent with actual obligations.
      Copy a verbatim excerpt of at least 20 words from the vendor's privacy
      page that states their commitment.

      Look specifically for: availability of a Data Processing Agreement (DPA),
      data residency options, right-to-deletion commitments, and whether their
      published guidance references are current.

      Vendors to check: Slack, Zoom, Atlassian, Notion, HubSpot, Dropbox,
      Salesforce, ServiceNow, Okta, DocuSign, Box, Monday.com, Workday,
      Zendesk, Twilio, Snowflake, Datadog, Cloudflare, PagerDuty, Elastic,
      Confluent, MongoDB, GitLab, Airtable, Figma.

      Write /workspace/findings_privacy.json as a JSON array with one object
      per vendor containing: vendor_name, gdpr_claim (string summary),
      ccpa_claim (string summary), dpa_available (true / false / unverified),
      privacy_evidence_url (URL to their privacy/DPA page),
      regulatory_alignment (Aligned / Partial / Unverified),
      verbatim_excerpt (20+ word direct quote from their privacy page),
      notes.

      Focus only on data privacy. Do not check SLA, certifications, incidents,
      or contract terms.

  - id: incident-history-specialist
    parallel_group: specialists
    depends_on: []
    subagent_type: explore
    description: >-
      You are the Incident History specialist. Your job is to find any publicly
      reported data breaches, significant outages, or security incidents
      involving each of the 25 vendors in the last 24 months.

      IMPORTANT: Do NOT rely on the vendor's current status page (e.g.
      status.vendor.com) â€” those show current uptime, not historical incidents.
      Instead, for each vendor use web search to find historical incidents:
      search for "[vendor name] data breach 2024", "[vendor name] security
      incident 2025", "[vendor name] outage 2024", and check the vendor's own
      security advisories page or blog. Also check the vendor's status page
      history section (past incidents tab) if available. Look for vendor
      security advisories, public breach notifications (HaveIBeenPwned,
      SEC filings, news reports). When you find an incident, copy a verbatim
      excerpt of at least 20 words from the source page describing it.

      Vendors to check: Slack, Zoom, Atlassian, Notion, HubSpot, Dropbox,
      Salesforce, ServiceNow, Okta, DocuSign, Box, Monday.com, Workday,
      Zendesk, Twilio, Snowflake, Datadog, Cloudflare, PagerDuty, Elastic,
      Confluent, MongoDB, GitLab, Airtable, Figma.

      Write /workspace/findings_incidents.json as a JSON array with one object
      per vendor containing: vendor_name, incident_found (true / false),
      incident_summary (brief description, or "None found in last 24 months"),
      incident_date (YYYY-MM if known, else null),
      incident_source_url (URL to the public disclosure or report, null if none),
      verbatim_excerpt (20+ word direct quote from the incident report, or
      "No excerpt available" if no incident found).

      If no incident is found after reasonable searching, write "None found in
      last 24 months" â€” do not leave the field blank.
      Focus only on incident history. Do not check SLA, certifications, privacy,
      or contract terms.

  - id: contract-risk-specialist
    parallel_group: specialists
    depends_on: []
    subagent_type: explore
    description: >-
      You are the Contract Risk specialist. Your job is to review the publicly
      available Terms of Service and Data Processing Agreements for all 25
      vendors and flag notable risk terms.

      For each vendor, find their publicly available Terms of Service or Master
      Service Agreement. Look specifically for:
      - Liability cap (capped at one month fees, one year, or uncapped?)
      - Data ownership clauses (does the vendor claim any rights to customer data?)
      - Exit/termination terms (notice period required, data export rights?)
      - Unilateral change rights (can vendor change terms without notice?)

      Vendors to check: Slack, Zoom, Atlassian, Notion, HubSpot, Dropbox,
      Salesforce, ServiceNow, Okta, DocuSign, Box, Monday.com, Workday,
      Zendesk, Twilio, Snowflake, Datadog, Cloudflare, PagerDuty, Elastic,
      Confluent, MongoDB, GitLab, Airtable, Figma.

      Write /workspace/findings_contracts.json as a JSON array with one object
      per vendor containing: vendor_name, tos_url (URL to their public ToS/MSA),
      liability_cap (string description), data_ownership_risk (High / Medium / Low),
      exit_terms_risk (High / Medium / Low), contract_risk_notes.

      Focus only on contract risk terms. Do not check SLA, certifications,
      privacy, or incidents.


  - id: evidence-compiler
    parallel_group: merger
    subagent_type: explore
    depends_on:
      - sla-performance-specialist
      - security-cert-specialist
      - privacy-compliance-specialist
      - incident-history-specialist
      - contract-risk-specialist
    description: >-
      You are the Evidence Compiler. Read the four domain specialist findings
      files for the evidence log: /workspace/findings_sla.json,
      /workspace/findings_certs.json, /workspace/findings_privacy.json,
      /workspace/findings_incidents.json. (The contract risk findings are
      handled separately by the Conflict Resolver.)

      Extract the evidence entries from each specialist's output and compile
      them into the evidence log. For each vendor and each domain (sla, certs,
      privacy, incidents), create one evidence entry containing: vendor_name,
      domain (exactly one of: sla, certs, privacy, incidents), claim_made
      (what the vendor claims or what was found), verification_url (the URL
      used to verify), verbatim_excerpt (the 20+ word direct quote from the
      specialist's findings â€” copy it exactly as the specialist recorded it).

      If a specialist wrote "No excerpt available" or did not include a
      verbatim excerpt for a vendor, carry that through â€” do not fabricate
      excerpts.

      IMPORTANT â€” entry ordering: Write entries interleaved by vendor, NOT
      grouped by domain. The correct order is: Slack-sla, Slack-certs,
      Slack-privacy, Slack-incidents, Zoom-sla, Zoom-certs, Zoom-privacy,
      Zoom-incidents, Atlassian-sla ... and so on through all 25 vendors.
      This ensures all 25 vendors appear distributed throughout the file, not
      clustered at the end.

      Before writing, verify that every one of the 25 vendors has at least one
      entry for each of the four domains (sla, certs, privacy, incidents) â€”
      that is 100 entries minimum covering all 25 vendors across all 4 domains.
      If any vendor is missing from a domain in the specialist files, create a
      placeholder entry with claim_made = "Not available" and verbatim_excerpt =
      "No excerpt available". Do not write any entry where verbatim_excerpt is
      fewer than 20 words unless it is exactly "No excerpt available" â€” if a
      specialist recorded a fragment shorter than 20 words, replace it with
      "No excerpt available" rather than carrying through a meaningless snippet.

      Write /logs/agent/evidence_log.json as a JSON array with at least 100
      entries (25 vendors x 4 domains). Each entry must have all five fields.

  - id: conflict-resolver
    parallel_group: merger
    subagent_type: explore
    depends_on:
      - sla-performance-specialist
      - security-cert-specialist
      - privacy-compliance-specialist
      - incident-history-specialist
      - contract-risk-specialist
    description: >-
      You are the Conflict Resolver. Read all five specialist findings files:
      /workspace/findings_sla.json, /workspace/findings_certs.json,
      /workspace/findings_privacy.json, /workspace/findings_incidents.json,
      /workspace/findings_contracts.json.

      Resolve boundary cases across specialist outputs:

      1. If a vendor has a reported incident but no corresponding mention in
         privacy findings, flag this as an unaddressed privacy gap.

      2. If a vendor claims ISO 27001 or SOC 2 but privacy findings show weak
         or unverified GDPR alignment, flag the inconsistency.

      3. If a vendor's contract shows High data_ownership_risk but their privacy
         claim appears strong, note the contradiction.

      4. If the same claim appears in both certs and privacy findings with
         conflicting status, keep the more conservative result and note it.

      Merge all five findings per vendor into one resolved record. Write
      /workspace/findings_resolved.json as a JSON array with one object per
      vendor containing all fields from all five specialist outputs, plus a
      conflict_notes field describing any resolved boundary cases.

      Then assign an overall_risk level per vendor: High if any cert "Not found"
      in registry, confirmed incident in last 24 months, data_ownership_risk
      High, or three or more Unverified claims. Medium if one or two Unverified
      claims, expired cert, moderate contract risk, or unsupported SLA. Low if
      everything checks out.

      Rank all 25 vendors 1 (highest risk) to 25 (lowest risk). Write
      /workspace/risk_ranking.json as a JSON array sorted by risk_rank with
      fields: vendor_name, risk_rank (integer 1-25), risk_level (High / Medium
      / Low), justification (one to two sentences citing specific findings from
      at least two of the four audit domains â€” for example: "SOC 2 registry
      shows 'Not found' (certs) and a confirmed June 2025 breach was reported
      (incidents)" or "SLA history shows 99.5% vs claimed 99.99% (sla) and
      GDPR DPA is missing (privacy)"; a risk ranking produced from a four-domain
      audit must reflect evidence from multiple domains, not a single data point).

  - id: final-assembler
    parallel_group: output
    subagent_type: explore
    depends_on:
      - conflict-resolver
      - evidence-compiler
    description: >-
      You are the Final Assembler. Read /workspace/findings_resolved.json and
      /workspace/risk_ranking.json.

      You must produce two files under /logs/agent/:

      First, produce /logs/agent/output.xlsx with exactly two sheets using
      openpyxl or xlsxwriter.

      Sheet 1 â€” vendor_audit (25 data rows, one per vendor, plus header row):
      Columns in order: vendor_name, sla_claim, sla_evidence_url, cert_claimed,
      cert_registry_status, privacy_claim, privacy_verified, incident_summary,
      incident_source_url, overall_risk, notes

      Sheet 2 â€” risk_ranking (25 data rows, one per vendor, plus header row):
      Columns in order: vendor_name, risk_rank, risk_level, justification

      Strict rules for the xlsx:
      - No blank cells â€” write "Unverified" for any field where data is missing
      - cert_registry_status must be exactly one of: Verified, Expired,
        Not found, Unverified
      - overall_risk must be exactly one of: High, Medium, Low
      - risk_rank values must be integers 1 through 25 with no duplicates

      Second, produce /logs/agent/risk_report.md as a written risk assessment
      between 2000 and 3000 words. Start with an executive summary of 200 to
      300 words covering the overall compliance posture across all 25 vendors.
      Then cover per-domain findings for SLA, certifications, privacy, and
      incidents, naming specific vendors. Call out the top five highest-risk
      vendors by name with specific evidence. Identify cross-vendor patterns.
      Close with a contract renewal action plan: for each of the five highest-risk
      vendors assign a renewal decision of Renew, Renegotiate, or Escalate to Legal,
      list the specific contract clauses or compliance gaps that drove that decision,
      and give a suggested sequence for handling them across Q3. Write in continuous
      prose with clear paragraph breaks, no markdown headings. Reference specific
      vendors throughout.

      Make sure the top five vendors in risk_ranking match the top five called
      out in the risk report.
```

```yaml
version: "1.0"
coordination_pattern: "hierarchical"

sub_tasks:
  - id: evidence-manager
    description: >
      Coordinate all source browsing and evidence extraction. Split the 50 pinned sources across
      source-reader workers. Produce a normalized evidence table with source_id, URL, title,
      publisher, source type, quote, topic, and incident relevance.
    depends_on: []
    parallel_group: evidence-tier

  - id: regulatory-source-readers
    description: >
      Read SEC, HHS, CMS, OCR, and other regulator/government sources. Extract exact quotes about
      disclosure, operational impact, provider support, breach response, and regulatory obligations.
    depends_on: [evidence-manager]
    parallel_group: source-reading

  - id: congressional-source-readers
    description: >
      Read congressional letters, hearing materials, testimony, and committee documents. Extract
      quotes about timeline, MFA/Citrix access, business impact, executive accountability, and
      healthcare-sector dependency risk.
    depends_on: [evidence-manager]
    parallel_group: source-reading

  - id: technical-advisory-readers
    description: >
      Read CISA/FBI/HHS advisories and public technical materials on ALPHV/BlackCat and ransomware
      mitigations. Extract quotes on TTPs, access methods, mitigations, and response controls.
    depends_on: [evidence-manager]
    parallel_group: source-reading

  - id: healthcare-impact-readers
    description: >
      Read healthcare association, provider impact, and financial stability sources. Extract quotes
      about claims, pharmacy, provider liquidity, patient-care disruption, and third-party systemic
      risk.
    depends_on: [evidence-manager]
    parallel_group: source-reading

  - id: chronology-manager
    description: >
      Consume verified evidence and reconstruct a factual incident chronology. Identify incident
      phases that can become exercise phases and mark which facts are suitable for injects.
    depends_on:
      - regulatory-source-readers
      - congressional-source-readers
      - technical-advisory-readers
      - healthcare-impact-readers
    parallel_group: synthesis-tier-1

  - id: exercise-design-manager
    description: >
      Convert the incident chronology into a 2-hour tabletop design. Define exercise objectives,
      phase structure, inject pacing, escalation logic, and role coverage requirements.
    depends_on: [chronology-manager]
    parallel_group: synthesis-tier-2

  - id: visual-exhibit-analyst
    description: >
      Interpret at least five real source-derived visual exhibits listed in visual_exhibits.json,
      including PDF charts, tables, figures, or control-taxonomy layouts. Produce
      visual_exhibit_notes.csv and identify which visual insights should shape the executive
      runbook and incident map.
    depends_on: [chronology-manager]
    parallel_group: synthesis-tier-2

  - id: inject-timeline-specialist
    description: >
      Draft injects_timeline.csv with at least 40 source-grounded injects. Every inject must include
      a valid source_id, source_url, quote, target role, expected decision, and debrief objective.
    depends_on: [exercise-design-manager]
    parallel_group: deliverable-tier-1

  - id: role-packet-manager
    description: >
      Coordinate six role packet writers and ensure every role packet references valid inject IDs,
      contains private role context, and aligns with the facilitator guide.
    depends_on: [exercise-design-manager, inject-timeline-specialist]
    parallel_group: deliverable-tier-2

  - id: ciso-role-writer
    description: "Write role_packets/ciso.md using verified inject IDs and evidence."
    depends_on: [role-packet-manager]
    parallel_group: role-writers

  - id: legal-role-writer
    description: "Write role_packets/legal.md using verified inject IDs and evidence."
    depends_on: [role-packet-manager]
    parallel_group: role-writers

  - id: communications-role-writer
    description: "Write role_packets/communications.md using verified inject IDs and evidence."
    depends_on: [role-packet-manager]
    parallel_group: role-writers

  - id: executive-role-writer
    description: "Write role_packets/executive_leadership.md using verified inject IDs and evidence."
    depends_on: [role-packet-manager]
    parallel_group: role-writers

  - id: it-operations-role-writer
    description: "Write role_packets/it_operations.md using verified inject IDs and evidence."
    depends_on: [role-packet-manager]
    parallel_group: role-writers

  - id: vendor-management-role-writer
    description: "Write role_packets/vendor_management.md using verified inject IDs and evidence."
    depends_on: [role-packet-manager]
    parallel_group: role-writers

  - id: facilitator-guide-writer
    description: >
      Write facilitator_guide.md with minute-by-minute instructions, delivery prompts, expected
      decisions, fallback prompts, and debrief notes tied to valid inject IDs.
    depends_on: [inject-timeline-specialist, role-packet-manager]
    parallel_group: deliverable-tier-3

  - id: executive-runbook-writer
    description: >
      Write executive_runbook.md for the CISO and exercise sponsor, including objectives, audience,
      schedule, staffing, assumptions, rules of engagement, remediation workflow, and visual exhibit
      insights from at least three valid exhibit IDs.
    depends_on: [exercise-design-manager, visual-exhibit-analyst]
    parallel_group: deliverable-tier-2

  - id: observer-scorecard-writer
    description: >
      Write observer_scorecards.csv. Every row must reference a valid inject_id and define objective,
      expected behavior, rating scale, evidence of decision quality, and improvement area.
    depends_on: [inject-timeline-specialist, role-packet-manager]
    parallel_group: deliverable-tier-3

  - id: incident-map-builder
    description: >

      systems/entities, dependencies, decision points, at least 20 inject IDs, and at least five
      visual exhibit IDs.
    depends_on: [chronology-manager, inject-timeline-specialist, visual-exhibit-analyst]
    parallel_group: deliverable-tier-2

  - id: after-action-template-writer
    description: >
      Write after_action_template.md with sections for strengths, gaps, owners, due dates,
      remediation actions, retest plan, and mappings back to observer objectives or inject IDs.
    depends_on: [observer-scorecard-writer, facilitator-guide-writer]
    parallel_group: deliverable-tier-4

  - id: evidence-register-writer

      Write evidence_register.csv from the verified evidence table. Include every source actually
      used, exact quoted evidence, artifact usage, and use purpose.
    depends_on:
      - inject-timeline-specialist
      - facilitator-guide-writer
      - ciso-role-writer
      - legal-role-writer
      - communications-role-writer
      - executive-role-writer
      - it-operations-role-writer
      - vendor-management-role-writer
      - executive-runbook-writer
      - visual-exhibit-analyst
      - observer-scorecard-writer
      - incident-map-builder
      - after-action-template-writer
    parallel_group: verification-tier-1

  - id: quote-grounding-verifier
    description: >
      Verify that every quoted_evidence string can be found in the matching source_text file.
      Flag any invented, mismatched, or overly short quotes.
    depends_on: [evidence-register-writer, inject-timeline-specialist]
    parallel_group: verification-tier-2

  - id: cross-reference-verifier
    description: >
      Check inject IDs, source IDs, role packet references, observer objectives, incident map nodes,
      and after-action references for consistency across all deliverables.
    depends_on:
      - inject-timeline-specialist
      - ciso-role-writer
      - legal-role-writer
      - communications-role-writer
      - executive-role-writer
      - it-operations-role-writer
      - vendor-management-role-writer
      - visual-exhibit-analyst
      - observer-scorecard-writer
      - incident-map-builder
      - after-action-template-writer
      - evidence-register-writer
    parallel_group: verification-tier-2

  - id: final-reconciler
    description: >
      Assemble the final output package in /logs/agent/output/. Resolve verifier findings, remove
      unsupported claims, fix broken cross-references, and ensure all required files are present.
    depends_on:
      - quote-grounding-verifier
      - cross-reference-verifier

      - facilitator-guide-writer

    parallel_group: final
```

--- REFERENCE 08 ---
Source file(s):
  documentations_phase_2\approved_sample_tasks\8654dfe186a74318b13b8d003486b62f-SWARMBENCH-HIERARCHICAL-PLANNING-OPERATIONS-CA-NORTHCOAST-WATER-DISCHARGE-ENFORCEMENT-CHRONOLOGY\decomposition.yaml
```yaml
coordination_pattern: hierarchical

# Genuine 3-tier hierarchy (dag_depth = 3, widest tier = 20):
#
#   Tier 1  - 20 readers (one scope each). Sixteen are pinned to a specific North Coast
#             Water Board enforcement document; four are cross-cutting specialists scoped
#             to a facet that genuinely spans several orders (supersession relationships,
#             the enforcement-escalation timeline, compliance deadlines, and the
#             discharger / parcel / waterway identifiers). Every reader reads its
#             document(s) from a fixed PDF URL on the pinned public mirror
#             (raw.githubusercontent.com, pinned to a commit SHA) - no reader depends on the
#             JavaScript adopted-orders search page or the board's bot-filtered site - and
#             writes grounded findings to its own shard under /logs/agent/.
#   Tier 2  - 3 consolidators. Each consumes only the reader shards in its group and writes
#             a consolidated shard plus a short notes file. This tier consumes Tier-1 output
#             before anything reaches the assembler, which is what makes the depth real
#             rather than a flat fan-out.
#   Tier 3  - 2 parallel assemblers that both consume the three consolidations: a
#             ledger-assembler that builds the chronology ledger, crosslink matrix,
#             contradiction log and the three charts, and a dedicated dossier-author that
#             writes the centerpiece site_dossier.docx. Splitting the narrative out keeps a
#             single overloaded assembler from dropping the most token-heavy deliverable.
#
# Every URL below is a direct, fetchable PDF on the pinned mirror (verified 200/real PDF).
# The same sixteen documents, with their official waterboards.ca.gov links, are listed in
# instruction.md, so the single agent and the multi-agent system start from identical
# information; this file only adds the coordination structure (who reads what, in what
# order). It contains no oracle values, answer-key content, or hints that shortcut the
# reading. Record either the mirror URL or the document's official waterboards.ca.gov URL as
# source_url in each row.
#
# Every reader writes its findings DIRECTLY to /logs/agent/ (never /workspace) so a partial
# run still leaves a scorable ledger. Ledger schema for every shard:
# finding_id,order_id,discharger,county,waterway,apn,event_date,topic,regulator_finding,
# quote,source_url. order_id is the order number (e.g. R1-2024-0044) or the file name where
# there is no clean number; quote is exact verbatim text from the order; regulator_finding
# is the reader's own paraphrase, different text from the quote.

sub_tasks:

  # ---------------- Tier 1, group A: Humboldt, Del Norte, and the relationship specialists ----------------

  - id: reader-larabee-0044
    parallel_group: read
    depends_on: []
    subagent_type: explore
    description: >-
      Read order R1-2024-0044 (Larabee Ranch Holdings LLC, Humboldt County),
      https://raw.githubusercontent.com/karthick-e1/northcoast-water-orders/574b8941112f212d853e75345998d1df37222bb0/orders/R1-2024-0044.pdf
      Read it end to end and write several distinct grounded findings for this order to
      /logs/agent/findings_larabee.csv in the ledger schema, spanning more than one topic.
      Each row carries one full-sentence verbatim quote from the order and a regulator_finding
      written in your own words that is different text from the quote; set the topic column to
      exactly one of the nine canonical topic labels in instruction.md, never an invented
      label.

  - id: reader-mcgaughey-0026
    parallel_group: read
    depends_on: []
    subagent_type: explore
    description: >-
      Read order R1-2024-0026 (Vern McGaughey and the Vern C. McGaughey Trust, Humboldt
      County),
      https://raw.githubusercontent.com/karthick-e1/northcoast-water-orders/574b8941112f212d853e75345998d1df37222bb0/orders/R1-2024-0026.pdf
      Read it end to end and write several distinct grounded findings for this order to
      /logs/agent/findings_mcgaughey.csv in the ledger schema, spanning more than one topic.
      Each row carries one full-sentence verbatim quote from the order and a regulator_finding
      written in your own words that is different text from the quote; set the topic column to
      exactly one of the nine canonical topic labels in instruction.md, never an invented
      label.

  - id: reader-lieberman-0047
    parallel_group: read
    depends_on: []
    subagent_type: explore
    description: >-
      Read order R1-2024-0047 (Aaron Lieberman, Paradise Valley LLC, Northland Management
      Group LLC and Northcoast Investment Group LLC, Humboldt County),
      https://raw.githubusercontent.com/karthick-e1/northcoast-water-orders/574b8941112f212d853e75345998d1df37222bb0/orders/R1-2024-0047.pdf

      part of it on the three consolidations (region_a_consolidated.csv,
      region_b_consolidated.csv, region_c_consolidated.csv and their notes) so the dossier

      swapped.
```

```yaml
sub_tasks:
  - id: analysis-leaf-act1
    parallel_group: leaf
    depends_on: []
    description: >-
      You are a leaf analyst owning ONLY act1 of the recap corpus; you cannot see any other act and must not spawn sub-agents. From /input_artifacts/recaps.jsonl take only the records whose "act" field is "act1". Cluster them so recaps describing the same fight moment share a group, and a moment only one witness described stands alone as its own group. For each recurring group (a beat) read its recaps to set the attacker (A=AKIRA, B=RYU-X), the closest move from [uppercut, straight, hook, elbow, palm, kick, knee, sweep], and a short callout. Act number is NOT fight order and your beats interleave with beats from other acts, so for each beat also record the momentum/position cues that locate it in the whole bout (who is ahead, who is worn down or pushed to an edge, early/middle/late feel). You do NOT know the absolute HP, combo, or timer (those depend on the whole fight, which you cannot see). Write /workspace/bundle_act1.json = {"assignments": [{"id": "<recap id>", "group": "act1_g1"}, ... every act1 recap exactly once, labels prefixed "act1_"], "beats": [{"group": "act1_g1", "attacker": "A", "move": "uppercut", "callout": "...", "momentum": "who is ahead / worn down", "phase": "early|middle|late"}, ... your act's beats]}.

Act number is NOT fight order and your beats interleave with beats from other acts, so for each beat also record the momentum/position cues that locate it in the whole bout (who is ahead, who is worn down or pushed to an edge, early/middle/late feel). You do NOT know the absolute HP, combo, or timer (those depend on the whole fight, which you cannot see).     description: >-
      You are the director at the top of the hierarchy. The four segment managers have each returned segment_1.json .. segment_4.json with their segment's ordered beats and a segment_momentum summary. Perform the GLOBAL reduction no single manager could: (1) MERGE the four segments into ONE global fight order by reasoning over their segment_momentum and the per-beat cues (segments and acts are NOT pre-sorted into fight order, so you must interleave them, not chain segment 1 then 2). (2) Resolve the continuity chain across that global order: start at full HP, combo 0, and the full timer, then walk every beat in order, decrementing the defender's HP by each blow and advancing the combo and timer, so each beat's start-state equals the previous beat's end-state regardless of which segment it came from (a per-segment manager cannot compute these absolute values). (3) Union every segment's assignments into the full recap-to-group map. Then render the reconstructed, continuity-resolved beats with the toolkit (render_fight in /input_artifacts/fightkit.py; see instruction.md) to /logs/agent/final.mp4 plus the per-shot clips. Write /logs/agent/output.json = {"assignments": [... all recaps, each exactly once ...], "beats": [{"group": "<label>", "attacker": "A", "move": "uppercut", "callout": "...", "hp_a": 900, "hp_b": 820, "combo": 3, "timer": 88}, ... in GLOBAL fight order]} and /logs/agent/manifest.json (one record per shot: {"order", "group", "clip"}).
```

  documentations_phase_2\approved_sample_tasks\a3f9c20e7b1d44856ce0d9f3a812b7c4-SWARMBENCH-HIERARCHICAL-PLANNING-OPERATIONS-TRIAL-MONITORING-VISIT-SCHEDULE\decomposition.yaml

```yaml
coordination_pattern: hierarchical

sub_tasks:

  - id: risk-northeast-manager
    parallel_group: collect
    depends_on: []
    description: >-
      You own the Northeast portfolio: Johns Hopkins (S09-S12), Massachusetts General
      Hospital (S17-S20), and Mount Sinai (S25-S28) - 12 sites. Spin up six workers in
      parallel, two sites each. Give every worker the scoring rules: from each site's current
      ClinicalTrials.gov record add 3 if the trial is suspended, terminated, or withdrawn;
      add 2 if it is recruiting but not updated since before 2025-06-01; add 2 if it is
      completed with no results posted; add 1 if the primary completion date is before
      2026-06-01 while still recruiting or active. Keep the four parts separate and sum them,
      and capture the study type (modality) and a source URL. Each worker writes
      /workspace/sites/{site_id}.json. Once all six finish, confirm all 12 files exist and
      write /workspace/regions/northeast.json with the complete and missing site lists.

  - id: risk-midwest-manager
    parallel_group: collect
    depends_on: []
    description: >-
      You own the Midwest portfolio: Cleveland Clinic (S01-S04), Mayo Clinic (S21-S24), and
      University of Michigan (S41-S44) - 12 sites. Spin up six workers in parallel, two sites
      each, and give every worker the same scoring rules used across the project (status 3,
      stale-recruiting 2, completed-no-results 2, overdue-primary-completion 1, kept as four
      separate parts and summed), plus the study type (modality) and a source URL per site.
      Each worker writes /workspace/sites/{site_id}.json. When all six finish, confirm all 12
      files exist and write /workspace/regions/midwest.json with the complete and missing
      site lists.

  - id: risk-south-manager
    parallel_group: collect
    depends_on: []
    description: >-
      You own the South portfolio: Duke University (S05-S08), MD Anderson Cancer Center
      (S13-S16), and Vanderbilt University (S45-S48) - 12 sites. Spin up six workers in
      parallel, two sites each, with the same four scoring rules (status 3, stale-recruiting
      2, completed-no-results 2, overdue-primary-completion 1, kept separate and summed) plus
      modality and a source URL per site. Each worker writes /workspace/sites/{site_id}.json.
      When all six finish, confirm all 12 files exist and write /workspace/regions/south.json
      with the complete and missing site lists.

  - id: risk-west-manager
    parallel_group: collect
    depends_on: []
    description: >-
      You own the West portfolio: Stanford University (S29-S32), UCLA (S33-S36), and UCSF
      (S37-S40) - 12 sites. Spin up six workers in parallel, two sites each, with the same
      four scoring rules (status 3, stale-recruiting 2, completed-no-results 2,
      overdue-primary-completion 1, kept separate and summed) plus modality and a source URL
      per site. Each worker writes /workspace/sites/{site_id}.json. When all six finish,
      confirm all 12 files exist and write /workspace/regions/west.json with the complete and
      missing site lists.

  - id: compliance-screen
    parallel_group: collect
    depends_on: []
    description: >-
      You own the regulatory screen. For each of the 12 partner institutions on the roster,
      check the institution against the FDA warning-letter list and the HHS OIG exclusions
      list and record what you find (or that nothing was found), with a source URL for each
      result. This screen does not change any risk_score; it is evidence for the audit trail.
      Write the findings keyed by institution to /workspace/compliance.json.

  - id: schedule-optimizer
    parallel_group: plan
    depends_on:
      - risk-northeast-manager
      - risk-midwest-manager
      - risk-south-manager
      - risk-west-manager
    description: >-
      Read the per-site risk files under /workspace/sites/ (all 48 sites) and choose the visit
      plan that satisfies the budget and fairness rules in the brief (at most 18 visits, at
      most 30 travel nights, every partner covered) while maximizing risk coverage per travel
      night and applying the stated tie-breakers. Write the chosen set with ranks and per-site
      objective contribution to /workspace/selection.json, and produce
      /logs/agent/monitoring_schedule.csv (the selected sites only, with the columns listed in
      the brief).

  - id: audit-assembler
    parallel_group: assemble
    depends_on:
      - risk-northeast-manager
      - risk-midwest-manager
      - risk-south-manager
      - risk-west-manager
      - compliance-screen
      - schedule-optimizer
    description: >-
      Merge the per-site risk files under /workspace/sites/, the compliance findings, and the
      chosen selection into /logs/agent/audit_trace.csv with one row for every roster site
      (trial fields checked, FDA/OIG results, the four score parts, total risk_score, selected
      flag, and source URLs). Also produce /logs/agent/constraint_checks.csv showing the caps
      used and whether the plan passed each check: visit count, travel nights, partner
      coverage, one visit per site, and roster coverage.

  - id: rationale-writer
    parallel_group: write
    depends_on:
      - schedule-optimizer
      - audit-assembler
    description: >-
      Using the selection and the audit record, produce /logs/agent/scheduling_rationale.docx:
      a short memo explaining how the schedule was chosen, which sites were left out because of
      the limits, how ties were handled, and what tradeoffs were made so every partner still
      got covered.
```

```yaml
coordination_pattern: "hierarchical"
sub_tasks:
- id: data-reconciliation-steward
  depends_on: []
  parallel_group: ingest
  description: >-
    You own the canonical route table the whole analysis is built on. Read
    /input_artifacts/title_vi_equity_policy.md first, then

    service_calendar_fy2026.csv. Build one canonical row per route with the
    governing post-change daily trips. Where the GTFS export disagrees with the
    adopted proposal, the proposal governs and you log the discrepancy. For each
    route compute the percent change in daily revenue miles and in daily trips on
    the affected day type and decide whether it is a major service change on its
    own (the 25 percent screen). Mark which routes have an actual change, and
    using the ACS block-group overlap field flag any route below 50 percent
    overlap as set-aside-for-data-quality (excluded from the equity rollup but
    still disclosed). Per the policy's aggregate rule, every changed route with
    adequate ACS coverage is included in the rollup regardless of whether it is
    individually major. Determine the annual-trips factor for each route from the
    service calendar. Emit a canonical route table (route_id, route_name,
    change_type, affected_day_type, daily_trips_after_governing,
    gtfs_conflict_flag, daily_trips_pct_change, is_major_change, has_change,
```
